<div class='question subquestion'>

Calculate the angles marked $x$ and $y$ in the diagram below

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


ABBR: $\text{Angles on a straight line add up to} = 180 \degree$.


ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a quadrilateral} = 360 \degree$


As per the daigram,

$
\begin{aligned}
x + 133 &= 180 \\\\
x       &= 180 - 133 \\\\
x       &= 47 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

As per the daigram,

$
\begin{aligned}
47 + 90 + y + 100 &= 360 \\\\
y + 237           &= 360 \\\\
y                 &= 360 - 237 \\\\
y                 &= 123 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$47 \degree, \ 123 \degree$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_of_triangle

rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_on_a_straight_line

rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_of_quadrilateral

$
\begin{aligned}
x + 133 &= 180 \\\\
x       &= 180 - 133 \\\\
x       &= 47 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
47 + 90 + y + 100 &= 360 \\\\
y + 237           &= 360 \\\\
y                 &= 360 - 237 \\\\
y                 &= 123 \degree
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Angles on a straight line add up to} = 180 \degree$.


ABBR: $\text{Angles around a point add up to} = 360 \degree$


As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
p + 48  &= 180 \\\\
p       &= 180 - 48 \\\\
p       &= 132 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

As it is an isosceles triangle, angle $r= q$

As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
q + 48 + r  &= 180 \\\\
q + q       &= 180 - 48 \\\\
2 q         &= 132 \\\\
q           &= \dfrac {132} {2} \\\\
q           &= \dfrac {66 \times 2} {2} \\\\
q           &= \dfrac {66 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
q           &= 66 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

$r = q = 66 \degree$

As $r$ and $t$ are opposite angles,

$t = r = 66 \degree$

Similarly,

u will be equal to s.

As we know, the sum of a complete angle is $360 \degree$. So,

$
\begin{aligned}
u + r + s + t    &= 360 \\\\
s + 66 + s + 66  &= 360 \\\\
2 s + 132        &= 360 \\\\
2 s              &= 360 - 132 \\\\
2 s              &= 228 \\\\
s                &= \dfrac {228} {2} \\\\
s                &= \dfrac {114 \times 2} {2} \\\\
s                &= \dfrac {114 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
s                &= 114 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

$\text {s} = \text {u} = 114 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$132 \degree, \ 66 \degree, \  66 \degree, 114 \degree, \ 66 \degree, \ 114 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_on_a_straight_line

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Sean scored $27$ out of $45$ in a test. Write this as a percentage.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Percentage} &= \dfrac {27} {45} \times 100 \\\\
                   &= \dfrac {27 \times 100} {45} \\\\
                   &= \dfrac {9 \times 3 \times 5 \times 20} {9 \times 5} \\\\
                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {9} \times 3 \times \cancel {5} \times 20} {\cancel {9} \times \cancel {5}} \\\\
                   &= 3 \times 20 \\\\
                   &= 60 \%
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$60 \%$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Percentage} &= \dfrac {27} {45} \times 100 \\\\
                   &= \dfrac {27 \times 100} {45} \\\\
                   &= \dfrac {9 \times 3 \times 5 \times 20} {9 \times 5} \\\\
                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {9} \times 3 \times \cancel {5} \times 20} {\cancel {9} \times \cancel {5}} \\\\
                   &= 3 \times 20 \\\\
                   &= 60 \%
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$60 \%$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

In a class of $30$ pupils, $21$ are girls. What percentage of the class is boys?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of boys}      &= 30 - 21 \\\\
                            &= 9 \\\\
\text {\% of boys}          &= \dfrac {9} {30} \times 100 \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {9 \times 100} {30} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {3 \times 3 \times 10 \times 10} {3 \times 10} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {3} \times 10 \times \cancel {10}} {\cancel {3} \times \cancel {10}} \\\\
                            &= 30 \%
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$30 \%$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

                            &= 9 \\\\
\text {\% of boys}          &= \dfrac {9} {30} \times 100 \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {9 \times 100} {30} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {3 \times 3 \times 10 \times 10} {3 \times 10} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {3} \times 10 \times \cancel {10}} {\cancel {3} \times \cancel {10}} \\\\
                            &= 30 \%
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Rearrange the following in order of size, **smallest to largest**.

$\dfrac{9}{25}, 0.371, \dfrac{2}{5}, 38\%$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

To compare, we can convert all the numbers to fractions, decimals or percentages. 

Let's convert all the numbers into decimals.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {9} {25}, \quad 0.371,  \quad \dfrac {2} {5},  \quad 38 \% \\\\
&= \dfrac {9 \times 4} {25 \times 4}, \quad 0.371,  \quad \dfrac {2 \times 2} {5 \times 2},  \quad \dfrac {38} { 100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {36} {100}, \quad 0.371,  \quad \dfrac {4} {10},  \quad \dfrac {38} { 100} \\\\
&= 0.36, \quad 0.371,  \quad 0.4,  \quad 0.38
\end{aligned}
$

Let's put them in ascending order now.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 0.36, \quad 0.371, \quad 0.38, \quad 0.4 \\\\
&= \dfrac {9} {25}, \quad 0.371, \quad 38 \%,  \quad \dfrac {2} {5}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {9} {25}, \quad 0.371, \quad 38 \%,  \quad \dfrac {2} {5}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>To compare, we can convert all the numbers to fractions, decimals or percentages. 

Let's convert all the numbers into decimals.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {9} {25}, \quad 0.371,  \quad \dfrac {2} {5},  \quad 38 \% \\\\
&= \dfrac {9 \times 4} {25 \times 4}, \quad 0.371,  \quad \dfrac {2 \times 2} {5 \times 2},  \quad \dfrac {38} { 100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {36} {100}, \quad 0.371,  \quad \dfrac {4} {10},  \quad \dfrac {38} { 100} \\\\
&= 0.36, \quad 0.371,  \quad 0.4,  \quad 0.38
\end{aligned}
$

Let's put them in ascending order now.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 0.36, \quad 0.371, \quad 0.38, \quad 0.4 \\\\
&= \dfrac {9} {25}, \quad 0.371, \quad 38 \%,  \quad \dfrac {2} {5}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the table,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Difference}  &= 4 - (-3) \\\\
                    &= 4 + 3 \\\\
                    &= 7 \degree \text {C}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

<div class='answer'>

$7 \degree \text {C}$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

He realises his thermometer is recording incorrectly and that each temperature should be $5 \degree \text{C}$ lower than was recorded. 
What is the correct temperature at $1 \ \text{am}$ on Monday?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$

<div class='question subquestion'>

On Wednesday the forecast is for all temperatures to drop by $3 \degree \text{C}$ from what they were on Tuesday.

</div>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Temperature at 2 am on Wednesday} &= -1 - 5 - 3 \\\\
                                         &= - 9 \degree \text {C}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

$3x = 36$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
3x &= 36 \\\\
x  &= \dfrac {36} {3} \\\\
x  &= \dfrac {12 \times 3} {3} \\\\
x  &= \dfrac {12 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
x  &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12$

</div>
</div>

x  &= \dfrac {12 \times 3} {3} \\\\
x  &= \dfrac {12 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
x  &= 12
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
4p - 2 &= 14 \\\\
4p     &= 14 + 2 \\\\
4p     &= 16 \\\\
p      &= \dfrac {16} {4} \\\\
p      &= \dfrac {4 \times 4} {4} \\\\
p      &= \dfrac {4 \times \cancel {4}} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
p      &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-7EFE6F8B-568B-4697-AAD0-42218DA1A407'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

The perimeter of a rectangle is $22 \ \text{cm}$. One of the sides has length $8 \ \text{cm}$. 
Calculate the lengths of the other three sides.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of rectangle} = 2 \times (\text{length} + \text{width})$


Let the width $= w$ 

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Perimeter of rectangle}      &= 22 \\\\
2 \times (8 + w)                    &= 22 \\\\
16 + 2 \times w                     &= 22 \\\\
2 \times \text w                    &= 22 - 16 \\\\
2 \times \text w                    &= 6 \\\\
w                                   &= \dfrac {6} {2} \\\\
w                                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 3} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
w                                   &= 3 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Other three sides of rectangle are 3cm, 3cm, 8cm.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\text {3cm, \ 3cm, \ 8cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_expanded_perimeter_rectangle

Let the width $= w$ 

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Perimeter of rectangle}      &= 22 \\\\
2 \times (8 + w)                    &= 22 \\\\
16 + 2 \times w                     &= 22 \\\\
2 \times \text w                    &= 22 - 16 \\\\
2 \times \text w                    &= 6 \\\\
w                                   &= \dfrac {6} {2} \\\\
w                                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 3} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
w                                   &= 3 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Other three sides of rectangle are 3cm, 3cm, 8cm.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\text {3cm, \ 3cm, \ 8cm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-D6E1FA30-6550-4FDE-A5F4-BCFFED7E0945'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

The contents of part of a wardrobe are shown in the pie chart below:

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


What fraction, in its simplest form, of **Footwear** are boots? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Fraction of Boots}       &= \dfrac {\text {Boots}} {\text {Total Footwear}} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {9} {63+9}    \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {9} {72}      \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel{9}} {8 \times \cancel{9}}    \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {1} {8}   
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {1} {8}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Fraction of Boots}       &= \dfrac {\text {Boots}} {\text {Total Footwear}} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {9} {63+9}    \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {9} {72}      \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel{9}} {8 \times \cancel{9}}    \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {1} {8}   
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {1} {8}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The prime factors for 66, 30 are:}\\\\
66&=2\times3\times11\\\\
30&=2\times 3\times5\\\\
\text{The factors that given numbers have in common are:}\\\\
&=2\times3\\\\
&=6
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find the lowest common multiple (LCM) of $12$ and $8$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The prime factors for 12, 8 are:}\\\\
12&=2\times2\times3\\\\
8&=2\times 2\times2\\\\
\text{Multiply each factor the greater number of times}\\
\text{it occurs in steps i) or ii) above to find the LCM:}\\\\
&=2\times2\times2\times 3\\\\
&=24
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-D8CD86EC-CEA6-473B-8C9B-8CCE13207DB6'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Fazal has $\pounds 2.60$ and Gareth has $\pounds 5.30$. Gareth gives Fazal some $5 \text{p}$ coins. 
Each boy now has the same amount of money. How many $5 \text{p}$ coins did Gareth give Fazal?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Let coins gave} &= x \\\\
\text{money gave}&= x \times 5 \ \text{p} \\\\
\text{Equation,}\\\\
530 - 5 \times x &= 260 + 5 \times x\\\\
530 - 260 &= 10 \times x\\\\
x &= 10
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

TODOWORKING

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

10

</div>
<div class='answer'>

TODOANSWER

</div>
</div>

\text{Equation,}\\\\
530 - 5 \times x &= 260 + 5 \times x\\\\
530 - 260 &= 10 \times x\\\\
x &= 10
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Which is the greater?

$3 \times 2^3$ or $2 \times 3^2$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
&= 3 \times 2^3 \\\\
&= 3 \times 8 \\\\
&= 24
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin {aligned}
&= 2 \times 3^2 \\\\
&= 2 \times 9 \\\\
&= 18
\end{aligned}
$

Larger number $=3 \times 2^3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$=3 \times 2^3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
&= 3 \times 2^3 \\\\
&= 3 \times 8 \\\\
&= 24
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin {aligned}
&= 2 \times 3^2 \\\\
&= 2 \times 9 \\\\
&= 18
\end{aligned}
$

Larger number $=3 \times 2^3$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$=3 \times 2^3$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

By how much?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
&= 24 - 18 \\\\
&= 6
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-01216F9C-A7F5-4791-AC06-D99D4185E47A'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

If: $\dfrac{3}{x} \times \dfrac{4}{7} = \dfrac{3}{14}$ 

Calculate the value of $x$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {3} {x} \times \dfrac {4} {7}  &= \dfrac {3} {14} \\\\
\dfrac {3 \times 4} {x \times 7}      &= \dfrac {3} {14} \\\\
\dfrac {1} {x}                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 7} {3 \times 4 \times 14} \\\\
x                                     &= \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 14} {3 \times 7} \\\\
x                                     &= \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 7 \times 2} {3 \times 7} \\\\
x                                     &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 4 \times \cancel {7} \times 2} {\cancel {3} \times \cancel {7}} \\\\  
x                                     &= 8
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {3} {x} \times \dfrac {4} {7}  &= \dfrac {3} {14} \\\\
\dfrac {3 \times 4} {x \times 7}      &= \dfrac {3} {14} \\\\
\dfrac {1} {x}                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 7} {3 \times 4 \times 14} \\\\
x                                     &= \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 14} {3 \times 7} \\\\
x                                     &= \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 7 \times 2} {3 \times 7} \\\\
x                                     &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 4 \times \cancel {7} \times 2} {\cancel {3} \times \cancel {7}} \\\\  
x                                     &= 8
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-16D089A8-F897-466C-A647-02A29D9F4503'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

If a basketball weighs $300 \ \text{g}$ plus half its own weight, how much does it weigh?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the weight of basketball $= b$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Weight of the basketball}                  &= 300 + \dfrac {1} {2} \times b \\\\
b                                                 &= 300 + \dfrac {b} {2} \\\\
b - \dfrac {b} {2}                                &= 300 \\\\
\dfrac {b \times 2} {1 \times 2} - \dfrac {b} {2} &= 300 \\\\
\dfrac {2 b} {2} - \dfrac {b} {2}                 &= 300 \\\\
\dfrac {2 b - b} {2}                              &= 300 \\\\
\dfrac {b} {2}                                    &= 300 \\\\
b                                                 &= 300 \times 2 \\\\
b                                                 &= 600 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$600 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the weight of basketball $= b$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Weight of the basketball}                  &= 300 + \dfrac {1} {2} \times b \\\\
b                                                 &= 300 + \dfrac {b} {2} \\\\
b - \dfrac {b} {2}                                &= 300 \\\\
\dfrac {b \times 2} {1 \times 2} - \dfrac {b} {2} &= 300 \\\\
\dfrac {2 b} {2} - \dfrac {b} {2}                 &= 300 \\\\
\dfrac {2 b - b} {2}                              &= 300 \\\\
\dfrac {b} {2}                                    &= 300 \\\\
b                                                 &= 300 \times 2 \\\\
b                                                 &= 600 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$600 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-802FD785-C7DC-4126-81E0-705D4DF84CA3'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

The centre of a square is at the point $(1, 2)$ and one of the vertices (corners) of the square is at the point $(4, 7)$.

</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-2B60E137-B7D3-4928-80E0-5B838ED84D0C'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Find in terms of $x$ the perimeter of the following triangle:

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Perimeter of triangle} &= (4x - 3) + (2x + 1) + (5 - 2x) \\\\
                              &= (4x + 2x - 2x) + (- 3 + 1 + 5) \\\\
                              &= 4x + 3
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4x + 3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Perimeter of triangle} &= (4x - 3) + (2x + 1) + (5 - 2x) \\\\
                              &= (4x + 2x - 2x) + (- 3 + 1 + 5) \\\\
                              &= 4x + 3
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

An egg box holds $6$ eggs. How many boxes are needed for $100$ eggs?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Boxes needed for 100 eggs} &= \dfrac {100} {6} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {50 \times 2} {2 \times 3} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {50 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2} \times 3} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {50} {3} \\\\
                                  &= 16.66
\end{aligned}
$

17 boxes are required for 100 eggs

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$17$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Boxes needed for 100 eggs} &= \dfrac {100} {6} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {50 \times 2} {2 \times 3} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {50 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2} \times 3} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {50} {3} \\\\
                                  &= 16.66
\end{aligned}
$

17 boxes are required for 100 eggs
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$17$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-D3A08968-A69A-4BA3-95F9-C69A858F5630'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

One of the angles of an isosceles triangle is $96 \degree$ . Find the sizes of the other two angles.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


Let the missing angle $= a$

As the triangle is an isosceles triangle, the third angle is also $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + a + 96      &= 180  \\\\
2a + 96         &= 180  \\\\
2a              &= 180 - 96  \\\\
2a              &= 84  \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {84} {2}  \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {42 \times 2} {2}  \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {42 \times \cancel{2}}{\cancel{2}}   \\\\
a               &= 42 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

Other two angles $= 42 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$42 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_of_triangle

Let the missing angle $= a$

As the triangle is an isosceles triangle, the third angle is also $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + a + 96      &= 180  \\\\
2a + 96         &= 180  \\\\
2a              &= 180 - 96  \\\\
2a              &= 84  \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {84} {2}  \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {42 \times 2} {2}  \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {42 \times \cancel{2}}{\cancel{2}}   \\\\
a               &= 42 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

Other two angles $= 42 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$42 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-03E012B1-67E0-4542-B1B2-6F0B3069FFA8'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Mrs Jones is $24$ years older than her daughter. The sum of their ages is $70$ years. How old is Mrs Jones?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let Mrs Jones age $=a$

Her daughter's age $= a - 24$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + a - 24 &= 70 \\\\
2 a        &= 70 + 24 \\\\
2 a        &= 94 \\\\
a          &= \dfrac {94} {2} \\\\
a          &= \dfrac {47 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a          &= \dfrac {47 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a          &= 47 \ \text {years}
\end{aligned}
$

Mrs Jones age $= 47 \ \text {years}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$47 \ \text {years}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let Mrs Jones age $=a$

Her daughter's age $= a - 24$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + a - 24 &= 70 \\\\
2 a        &= 70 + 24 \\\\
2 a        &= 94 \\\\
a          &= \dfrac {94} {2} \\\\
a          &= \dfrac {47 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a          &= \dfrac {47 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a          &= 47 \ \text {years}
\end{aligned}
$

Mrs Jones age $= 47 \ \text {years}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$47 \ \text {years}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-67CE5EA7-B84F-48C9-902F-F787EE74BD8A'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A metal rod is $10 \dfrac{4}{5} \ \text{metres}$ long.  How many short rods $\dfrac{3}{10} \ \text{metres}$ 
long can be cut from the longer rod?  

**Show full working**.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Numbers of rods} &= \dfrac {\text {Length of longer rod}} {\text {Length of shorter rod}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {10 \dfrac {4} {5}} {\dfrac {3} {10}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\dfrac {54} {5}} {\dfrac {3} {10}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {54} {5} \times \dfrac {10} {3} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {54 \times 10} {5 \times 3} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 18 \times 5 \times 2} {5 \times 3} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 18 \times \cancel {5} \times 2} {\cancel {5} \times \cancel {3}} \\\\
                        &= 18 \times 2 \\\\
                        &= 36
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$36$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Numbers of rods} &= \dfrac {\text {Length of longer rod}} {\text {Length of shorter rod}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {10 \dfrac {4} {5}} {\dfrac {3} {10}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\dfrac {54} {5}} {\dfrac {3} {10}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {54} {5} \times \dfrac {10} {3} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {54 \times 10} {5 \times 3} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 18 \times 5 \times 2} {5 \times 3} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 18 \times \cancel {5} \times 2} {\cancel {5} \times \cancel {3}} \\\\
                        &= 18 \times 2 \\\\
                        &= 36
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$36$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Two different clocks show the time $3 \ \text{o'clock}. The first gains $5 \ \text{minutes}$ per hour and the second gains 
$20 \ \text{minutes}$ per hour. How long will it be in hours before both clocks look as though they show the same time?

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-9B6496FA-879D-4143-A8F4-4F7EC16CCC67'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A *double decker* sandwich has three slices of bread and two layers of filling. 
(e.g. bread/filling/bread/filling/bread). 
Each slice of bread has to be buttered on each side that is in contact with the filling. 
I make as many of these sandwiches as possible from a sliced loaf which has $22$ usable slices, 
excluding crusts which are not used. How many sides of bread do I have to butter?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{breads} &= 22-2      \\\\
\text{sandwich} &= 20 \div 3 \\\\
&\approx 6 \\\\
\text{sides} &= 24 
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

TODOWORKING

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

24

</div>
<div class='answer'>

TODOANSWER

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

\text{sandwich} &= 20 \div 3 \\\\

\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
